GWC 000629.09 ΓÇö Internal Communication Fault for 5 
Seconds
A See & Spray control unit or units encountered an internal communication fault.
NOTE:
If this issue persists for longer than 30 seconds, the control unit will perform a reboot in which
case diagnostic trouble code GWC 00629.12 will become active.
The See & Spray control unit messages were missing for more than 5 seconds internally for the faulted
control unit or on the ExactApply subnet between the vision processing unit (VPU) and ExactApply nozzle
(NZC). The associated nozzles reverted to the selected fallback mode while the diagnostic trouble code
(DTC) was active.
Component Information:
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
W0080 ΓÇö Center Frame Dual Product Harness
W0087 ΓÇö Center Frame Sensing Harness
W0105 ΓÇö Left-Hand Inner Wing Sensing Harness
W0106 ΓÇö Right-Hand Inner Wing Sensing Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
See & Spray Power and Ground ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Verify that the code is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05).
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
1.
NOTE:
GWC 000629.xx DTCs should always show as inactive as the affected VPU performs a
reboot. If there are any other gateway control unit (GWC) related DTCs, diagnose these
first. This diagnostic procedure addresses all GWC 000629.xx DTCs.
Cycle the key switch to reboot the system, clear out error messages, and re-establish proper
communication messages.
2.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message counts are
changing on the faulted VPU control unit.
3.
If a single VPU is faulted and not showing message counts changing:

[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85c434ab]
GWC 000629.12 ΓÇö Internal Communication Fault for 30 
Seconds
A See & Spray control unit or units encountered an internal communication fault for 30 seconds
NOTE:
Diagnostic trouble code (DTC) GWC 00629.09 will turn into GWC 00629.12 after 30 seconds of
being active.
The See & Spray control unit messages were missing internally for the faulted control unit or on the
ExactApply subnet between the faulted vision processing unit (VPU) and ExactApply nozzle (NZC). The
associated nozzles reverted to the selected fallback mode.
Component Information:
Nozzle Assemblies (NZC)
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
Schematic:
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Verify that the code is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05).
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
1.
Cycle the key switch to reboot the system, clear out error messages, and re-establish proper
communication messages.
2.
Utilize Service ADVISOR to ensure that all control units have the proper software.
3.
Ensure that all control units are communicating on the applicable network or subnet.
4.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable CAN network (when available).
1.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message
counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Refer to the component locations. Visually check all connectors, components, and harnesses for
corrosion, binding, and damage.
5.
Identify and disconnect all CAN bus passive terminators, located on the appropriate CAN bus network.
6.
Each network should have 2 terminators.
1.
Verify that each terminator has 120 ╬⌐ of resistance across it.
2.
If a terminator does not have 120 ╬⌐, replace that terminator.
3.

GWC 000639.14 ΓÇö No CAN Bus Messages are Arriving
The controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) has encountered an issue.
The control unit has a communication fault.
Schematic:
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30D)
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Related information:
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇô Electrical Schematic( Section 240, Group 30D)
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇô Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
Overview: CAN and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic (Section 240, Group 30D)
Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
Verify that the diagnostic trouble code (DTC) is still active. Refer to Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
(Section 211, Group 05). If inactive, refer to 
 (Section
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
211, Group 05).
1.
Cycle the key switch to reboot the system, clear out error messages, and re-establish proper
communication messages.
2.
Verify the gateway control unit (GWC) is communicating on the applicable network or subnet.
3.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable CAN network (when available).
1.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message
counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Refer to the appropriate schematic and visually check all connectors, components, and harnesses for
corrosion, binding, and damage.
4.
Identify and disconnect all CAN bus passive terminators, located on the appropriate CAN bus network.
5.
Each network should have 2 terminators.
1.
Verify that each terminator has 120 ╬⌐ of resistance across it.
2.
If a terminator does not have 120 ╬⌐, replace that terminator.
3.
Reprogram the control unit or control units connected to this network or subnet. Refer toControl Unit
Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
6.
If power and ground are good and reprogramming was successful but the diagnostic trouble code
(DTC) returns, replace the control unit.
7.

7.
Visually verify that relevant fuses are not blown.
1.
Identify relevant power and ground/return circuits.
2.
Test for proper voltage on the potentially faulty component, between the power circuit and the ground
/return circuit.
8.
If the test between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit is bad, test for voltage
between the power circuit and a frame ground.
1.
If the test between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit is bad, but the test between
the power circuit and the frame ground is good, then the issue is on the ground/return circuit.
2.
If the test between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit is bad, and the test between
the power circuit and the frame ground is bad, then the issue is on the power circuit.
3.

[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85c4354e]
GWC 001235.11 ΓÇö Addressing Fault with Gateway Control 
Unit
One or more See & Spray vision processing control unit (VPU) has an addressing failure with the gateway 
control unit (GWC) on controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) 3.
The See & Spray VPUs cannot claim an address with GWC on the BoomTrac CAN bus. One or more faulted
See & Spray VPUs are disabled. Cycling power may re-establish communication.
Addressing requires proper functioning power, ground, addressing, and CAN circuits. Diagnose and repair
CAN circuits, starting with first faulted control units. Identify the fault location within the Diagnostic Center
application under See & SprayΓÇöImage Processor/Camera.
Component Information:
XA725 ΓÇö Gateway Control Unit (GWC) Connector
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
W0080 ΓÇö Center Frame Dual Product Harness
W0087 ΓÇö Center Frame Sensing Harness
W0105 ΓÇö Left-Hand Inner Wing Sensing Harness
W0106 ΓÇö Right-Hand Inner Wing Sensing Harness
Schematic:

Sync Fault
Check for a circuit fault on the sync line between the VPUs on the Address Sync In, Out, and Ground
circuits. The Address Sync circuits are pins C3, C4, D3, and D4 on the VPU connectors.

2.
If a flashing pattern of two purple flashes, pause, two purple flashes, pause, is witnessed, then

a solid green indicator light if the VPU is powered and connected to BoomTrac CAN.
1.
From the center of the machine, diagnose the issue between last VPU without an addressing fault and
the first VPU with an addressing fault. For example, if VPU 8 is online and VPUs 9 and 10 have
addressing faults, check the sync circuits between VPUs 8 and 9.

GWC 001235.14 ΓÇö See & Spray Control Unit Missing Data 
Input
Internal communication fault during startup. The See & Spray control unit reverted to fallback mode.
Confirm Work Setup is complete and a compatible crop type is selected. Confirm ExactApply (EA) setup is
configured for See & Spray, including selecting a compatible nozzle part number. The system requires
input from the StarFire receiver, boom height sensors, and inertial measurement unit (IMU) sensors. Verify
that the components are calibrated and perform troubleshooting on any active codes. Cycle power to
attempt recovery.

ΓÇó

ΓÇó

MATCH 'See & Spray Addressing' on PDF page 1548
======================================================================
1548
[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85c435d1]
GWC 001672.02 ΓÇö Sync Line Communication Failure
A See & Spray control unit has a sync line failure.
The See & Spray control unit has not received the addressing frequency signal from the adjacent See &
Spray control unit to the left. Addressing requires functioning sync circuits. The See & Spray system is
disabled.
Component Information:
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
W0087 ΓÇö Center Frame Sensing Harness
W0105 ΓÇö Left-Hand Inner Wing Sensing Harness
W0106 ΓÇö Right-Hand Inner Wing Sensing Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
There is a sync line fault. The likely cause is a power disruption to the most inboard vision processing unit
(VPU) that is not addressed or a harness failure between the last addressed VPU and the first non-
addressed VPU.
Related information:
See & Spray Addressing ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Verify that the code is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05).
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
1.
Ensure that all control units are communicating on the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable CAN network (when available).
1.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message
counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Identify which VPUs are online.
3.
If the VPUs are not shown as online, refer to the appropriate See & Spray schematic and diagnose the
power, ground, and controller area network (CAN) circuits for the VPUs that are not online, starting
with the VPUs closest to the ones that are online.
4.
Diagnose and repair the sync circuit at the first VPU reporting as faulted, going from left to right as
facing the rear of the machine.
5.
Check for a failed harness or faulty components between the last addressed VPU and the first non-
addressed VPU. Refer to the component locations. Visually check all connectors, components, and
harnesses for corrosion, binding, and damage. Repair or replace as necessary.
6.

[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85c435d4]
GWC 001672.11 ΓÇö Addressing Fault with GWC
A See & Spray control unit or units has an addressing failure with the gateway control unit (GWC).
The See & Spray control unit cannot claim an address with the GWC on controller area network (CAN)
binary unit system (bus) 7. More than one control unit is requesting the same address. See & Spray system
is disabled.
Schematic:
Boom CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30D)
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30D)
See & Spray AddressingΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Related information:
Boom CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50D)
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50D)
Verify that the code is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05).
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
1.
Cycle the key switch to reboot the system, clear out error messages, and re-establish proper
communication messages.
2.
Ensure that the GWC is communicating with all necessary control units on the implement CAN bus.
3.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable CAN network (when available).
1.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message
counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
If the GWC is not showing diagnostic addresses or very few are listed, diagnose any possible
implement CAN bus issues or BoomTrac CAN bus issues.
3.
Make note of which VPUs are online and which VPUs are offline. Verify if missing VPU was recently
added or reprogrammed.
4.
If the offline VPU is newly added, verify the payload version and that the payload was completely
loaded. Refer toVPU Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C) and attempt to reprogram VPU.
1.
If the offline VPU was recently reprogrammed, refer toVPU Reprogramming(Section 245, Group
10C) and attempt to reprogram the VPU.
2.
Refer to the electrical schematic. Narrow down CAN bus issues to the root cause. Refer to See & Spray
 (Section 240, Group 50L).
Addressing ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic
5.
Always visually check all connectors, components, and harnesses for corrosion, binding, and damage.
6.

GWC002157.09 ΓÇö Source Address 162 Message Missing
See & Spray control unit 1 (VPU1) was not detected. The gateway control unit (GWC) is no longer receiving 
messages from the See & Spray control unit. The associated nozzles reverted to the selected fallback mode.
NOTE:
At powerup, allow 2ΓÇö3 minutes for active diagnostic trouble codes (DTCs) to appear on the
display.
This DTC may change to 1235.11 after a key cycle. Diagnose any 21xx.09 DTCs first, then
address any 1235.11 DTCs.
The vision processing unit (VPU) must be communicating properly and then lose communication with the
GWC for this DTC to be active. This can be caused by loss of switched or unswitched power at pins L3/M3,
grounds at pins L4/M4, or BoomTrac controller area network (CAN) high/low at pins F3/F4 to the affected
VPU.
Viewing the camera feed after completing the addressing procedure at start-up is a quick way to isolate
whether the cause of the DTC is a power and ground issue or a CAN issue. If the camera feed is showing
properly for a faulted VPU, then power and ground to the camera with the key ON and the engine ON is
present.
The following are necessary for this diagnostic procedure:
Schematic:
Boom CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic (Section 240, Group 30D)
See & Spray Power and Ground ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
┬á
1┬á Procedure 1
Action:
Clear the fault codes, cycle power to the machine, then on the Generation 4 display:
Does the fault message display as active?
Result:
YES:
GO TO2
NO:

GWC 008269.05 ΓÇö Control Unit Ethernet Circuit Open
The See & Spray control unit detected an open Ethernet circuit.
See & Spray setting adjustments, high resolution mapping, camera video, and reprogramming are
disabled.
Component Information:
Ethernet Switch Assemblies
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
W0032 ΓÇö Cab Main Harness
W0085 ΓÇö Ethernet Assembly Center Frame Harness
W0107 ΓÇö Left-Hand Wing Ethernet Assembly Harness
W0108 ΓÇö Right-Hand Wing Ethernet Assembly Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Verify that the code is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
(Section 211, Group 05).
1.
Check the display and perform any calibrations necessary. Refer toCalibration Procedure Information
(Section 245, Group 10B).
2.
Refer to the component locations.
3.
Visually check all connectors, components, and harnesses for corrosion, binding, and damage.
1.
If possible, check for component functionality.
2.
Verify that harness and circuit routing matches the electrical schematic.
3.
Refer to the electrical schematic.
4.
Visually verify that relevant fuses are not blown.
1.
Identify relevant power, ground/return, and signal circuits.
2.
Identify relevant signal circuits.
3.
Test for voltage between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit.
5.
If the test between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit is bad, test for voltage
between the power circuit and a frame ground.
1.


======================================================================
MATCH 'See & Spray Ethernet' on PDF page 1581
======================================================================
1581
Related information:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20L)
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50L)
If the tests show the ground/return circuit is bad but the test with the frame ground is good, then
the issue is on the power circuit.
2.
If the test with the frame ground is bad, then the issue is on the ground circuit.
3.
If applicable, disconnect all connectors and components attached to the signal circuit. Perform an 
 (Section 240, Group 10) across the signal circuit.
Open Circuit Load Test
6.
Refer to the electrical schematic. Narrow down any circuit issues to the root cause. Refer to Circuit
 (Section 240, Group 10).
Checks
7.
Reprogram the control unit. Refer toControl Unit Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
8.
Refer to the electrical system diagnostic.
9.

GWC 516148.13 ΓÇö Camera Not Calibrated or Upside Down
A See & Spray camera is not calibrated or is mounted upside down.
The See & Spray control unit does not recognize the connected camera or during calibration the See &
Spray control unit interpreted the image to be rotated 180┬░ for the faulted camera. The See & Spray system
is disabled for the associated nozzles.

W0123 ΓÇö VPU 9 Camera Assembly Harness
W0124 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0125 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Outer Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:

[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85c23fc1]
GWC 516148.14 ΓÇö Camera Lens is Dirty or View is 
Obstructed
The region of interest on the camera lens is obstructed with dirt or debris.
This diagnostic trouble code (DTC) is only active when the vehicle speed is greater than 8 km/h (5 mph) and
can take up to two minutes to refresh status values.
Component Information:
Cameras
W0114 ΓÇö VPU 1 Assembly Camera Outer Harness
W0115 ΓÇö VPU 1 Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0116 ΓÇö VPU 2 Camera Assembly Harness
W0117 ΓÇö VPU 3 Camera Assembly Harness
W0118 ΓÇö VPU 4 Camera Assembly Harness
W0119 ΓÇö VPU 5 Camera Assembly Harness
W0121 ΓÇö VPU 7 Camera Assembly Harness
W0122 ΓÇö VPU 8 Camera Assembly Harness
W0123 ΓÇö VPU 9 Camera Assembly Harness
W0124 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0125 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Outer Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Utilize the camera feed to locate camera with obstruction. It may be possible to view what is causing
the obstruction. On the display, select Menu > System > Diagnostic Center > See & SprayΓÇöImage
Processor/Camera and select the desired camera.
1.
Once the faulted camera has been identified, visually inspect the camera.
NOTE:
If dust on a center frame camera is the cause of the obstruction, slowing the machine
speed will decrease dust from the wheels.
2.
Clean the faulted camera lens with a soft cloth. Microfiber cloth and a spray bottle filled with water is
preferred. A mild detergent such as dish soap may be used if there is grease on the lens.
Starting in the center of the lens, wipe the lens in a circular motion and gently remove oil, dirt, and
dust.
3.

the issue is on the power circuit.

MATCH 'See & Spray Ethernet' on PDF page 1598

1598
Related information:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20L)
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50L)
See & Spray Power and Ground ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50L)
See & Spray Power and Ground ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20L)
If all of the cameras and VPUs associated to one Ethernet switch are faulted, then the issue is with the
Ethernet switch.
To test the Ethernet switch, swap the potentially faulty Ethernet switch with a known properly
functioning Ethernet switch. Retest the See & Spray system. If the issue moves from the original
location, replace the faulted Ethernet switch.
1.
If the issue stays in the original location, swap the Ethernet switch harness at the faulted location
with a known properly functioning Ethernet switch harness. Retest the See & Spray system. If the
issue now moves from the original location, replace the faulty Ethernet switch harness.
2.
If all of the cameras associated to one VPU are faulted, then the issue is with the VPU.
7.
Swap the VPU at the faulted location with a VPU at a known properly functioning location. Retest
the See & Spray system. If the issue moves from the original location, replace the faulted VPU.
1.
If the issue stays in the original location, swap the VPU harness at the faulted location with a
known properly functioning VPU harness. Retest the See & Spray system. If the issue now moves
from the original location, replace the faulty VPU harness.
2.
If an individual camera is faulted, then the issue is with the camera.
8.
Swap the camera at the faulted location with a camera at a known properly functioning location.
Retest the See & Spray system. If the issue moves from the original location, replace the faulted
camera.
1.
If the issue stays in the original location, swap the camera harness at the faulted location with a
known properly functioning camera harness. Retest the See & Spray system. If the issue now
moves from the original location, replace the faulty camera harness.
2.

GWC 518216.02 ΓÇö Sync Line Communication Failure
The See & Spray control unit has not received the addressing frequency signal from the adjacent See & 
Spray control unit to the left.
The control unit has reverted to the last known address position to allow the See & Spray system to
function. Control units downstream may report as faulted. For replaced control units, an address line is
required for initial startup for See & Spray to function.

(VPU) that is not addressed or a harness failure between the last addressed VPU and the first non-
addressed VPU.

 (Section 211, Group 05). If

inactive, refer to

Ensure that all control units are communicating on the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable CAN network (when available).
1.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message
counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Identify which VPUs are online.
3.
If the VPUs are not shown as online, refer to the appropriate See & Spray schematic and diagnose the
power, ground, and controller area network (CAN) circuits for the VPUs that are not online, starting
with the VPUs closest to the ones that are online.
4.
Diagnose and repair the sync circuit at the first VPU reporting as faulted, going from left to right as
facing the rear of the machine.
5.
Check for a failed harness or faulty components between the last addressed VPU and the first non-
addressed VPU. Refer to the component locations. Visually check all connectors, components, and
harnesses for corrosion, binding, and damage. Repair or replace as necessary.
6.

Diagnostic Steps:
Related information:
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20L)
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50L)
Verify that the diagnostic trouble code (DTC) is still active. Refer to Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
(Section 211, Group 05). If inactive, refer to 
 (Section
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
211, Group 05).
1.

2.

1.

counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Cycle the key switch to attempt a system restart. Verify if the DTC is still present and active.

If the DTC is still present and active, reprogram the control unit. Refer toControl Unit
Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
1.
If the DTC is still present after reprogramming the control unit, contact the diagnostic technical
assistance center (DTAC). Refer to 
 (Section 210, Group 100).

1662
[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85c42921]
GWC 518244.02 ΓÇö Camera Not Transmitting Images
A See & Spray camera or cameras are not transmitting images.
The associated nozzles reverted back to the selected fallback mode.
Component Information:
Cameras
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
W0114 ΓÇö VPU 1 Assembly Camera Outer Harness
W0115 ΓÇö VPU 1 Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0116 ΓÇö VPU 2 Camera Assembly Harness
W0117 ΓÇö VPU 3 Camera Assembly Harness
W0118 ΓÇö VPU 4 Camera Assembly Harness
W0119 ΓÇö VPU 5 Camera Assembly Harness
W0121 ΓÇö VPU 7 Camera Assembly Harness
W0122 ΓÇö VPU 8 Camera Assembly Harness
W0123 ΓÇö VPU 9 Camera Assembly Harness
W0124 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0125 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Outer Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Cycle the key switch to attempt a system restart. Verify if the code is still present and active.
1.
If the code is still active, identify the fault location. On the display, select the Menu icon, System,
Diagnostic Center, and then See & SprayΓÇöImage Processor/Camera.
2.
Check the Fachkreis Automobil (FAKRA) harnesses and connections at the cameras and VPUs. Check
for loose connections and damage.
3.
Check the VPU diagnostic addresses for the faulted cameras.
NOTE:
On machines equipped with See & Spray Ultimate, only every other VPU port has a camera
connected.
On machines equipped with See & Spray Premium, a camera is connected to every VPU
port.
4.


======================================================================
MATCH 'See & Spray Ethernet' on PDF page 1663
======================================================================
1663
Related information:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20L)
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 240, Group 50L)
VPU ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic (Section 245, Group 50)
View VPU diagnostic addresses 15ΓÇö22. VPU port 1 is shown on odd-numbered diagnostic addresses.
VPU port 2 is shown on even-numbered diagnostic addresses. For example, VPU port 1 is shown on
diagnostic address (DA) 15. VPU port 2 is shown on DA 16.
Review the information listed for each VPU port. Address any issues based on the data given in the
camera diagnostic information.
Camera diagnostic information is listed for each VPU port as seven digits separated by commas. For
example, DA 15 = 1,1,2,3,0,0,0.
5.
First Digit = Expected Camera Connection
ΓÇó
0 = No Camera Expected on This Port
ΓÇó
1 = Camera Expected on This Port
ΓÇó
Second Digit = Camera Connection Status
ΓÇó
0 = No Camera Connected to This Port
ΓÇó
1 = Camera Connected to This Port
ΓÇó
Third Digit = Camera Current in Milliamps (mA)
ΓÇó
Fourth Digit = Link Health
ΓÇó
0 = Not Connected
ΓÇó
1 = Good Link
ΓÇó
2 = Fair Link
ΓÇó
3 - Bad Link
ΓÇó
Fifth Digit = Always a Value of 0
ΓÇó
Sixth Digit = Always a Value of 0
ΓÇó
Seventh Digit = Always a Value of 0
ΓÇó

MATCH 'See & Spray Ethernet' on PDF page 1664

2.

3.

5.

between the power circuit and a frame ground.
1.

the issue is on the power circuit.
2.
If the test with the frame ground is bad, then the issue is on the ground circuit.
3.

 (Section 240, Group 10) across the signal circuit.

GWC 518252.02 ΓÇö Control Unit Internal Processing Fault
One or more See & Spray control units encountered an internal processing fault.
The associated nozzles reverted back to selected fallback mode.
Component Information:
XA126 ΓÇö StarFire Global Satellite System (GPS) Receiver Connector
Cameras
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
Schematic:
Overview: CAN and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30D)
Diagnostic Steps:
Cycle the power to attempt recovery.
1.
Verify that the code is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05).
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
2.
If the code is still active, identify the fault location. On the display, select the Menu icon, System tab,
Diagnostic Center icon, System Diagnostics tab, and then See & SprayΓÇöImage Processor/Camera.
3.
Go to the diagnostic address for the faulted VPU. On the display, select the Menu icon, System tab,
Diagnostic Center icon, System Diagnostics tab, and then select the diagnostic address associated
with the faulted VPU.
4.
After selecting the diagnostic address for the faulted VPU, select diagnostic address 72. Review the
message found at diagnostic address 72.
5.
If the message states "This VPU runs partial SnAP" or "SnAP complete", it indicates that the VPU is
functioning properly. Check for other issues related to the faulted VPU. Refer to VPU ΓÇö Electrical
 (Section 245, Group 50) and 
Diagnostic
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic
(Section 240, Group 50L).
1.
If the message states "Inference has no model", it indicates a crop type that is not compatible
with See & Spray is selected.
To change the crop type, select the Menu icon, Machine Settings tab, and then select the See &
Spray icon. Next, select a crop type that is compatible with See & Spray.
NOTE:
The crop type can also be changed using the Work Setup display application.
2.
If the message states "Pose no origin", it indicates there is a machine position error or boom
position error.
3.

This diagnostic trouble code (DTC) appears when a camera has low current draw or an open circuit. If this
DTC is active at start-up and then goes inactive shortly after, this is not a functional issue and can be
ignored unless the DTC is still active. If a camera is unplugged, this DTC should be active. If a camera is
disconnected for diagnostic purposes, this DTC can be ignored.

GWC 518253.14 ΓÇö Camera on Unused Port Detected
The See & Spray control unit detects that there is a camera connected to an unused port.
The associated nozzles reverted back to the selected fallback mode.
Component Information:
Cameras
Vision Processing Unit (VPU) Assemblies
W0114 ΓÇö VPU 1 Assembly Camera Outer Harness
W0115 ΓÇö VPU 1 Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0116 ΓÇö VPU 2 Camera Assembly Harness
W0117 ΓÇö VPU 3 Camera Assembly Harness
W0118 ΓÇö VPU 4 Camera Assembly Harness
W0119 ΓÇö VPU 5 Camera Assembly Harness
W0121 ΓÇö VPU 7 Camera Assembly Harness
W0122 ΓÇö VPU 8 Camera Assembly Harness
W0123 ΓÇö VPU 9 Camera Assembly Harness
W0124 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Inner Harness
W0125 ΓÇö VPU A Assembly Camera Outer Harness
Schematic:
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Check the FAKRA harnesses and connections at the cameras and VPUs. Check the camera harness is
connected to the correct port on the VPU.
1.
Check the VPU diagnostic addresses for the faulted cameras.
NOTE:
On machines equipped with See & Spray Ultimate, only every other VPU port has a camera
connected.
On machines equipped with See & Spray Premium, a camera is connected to every VPU
port.
View VPU diagnostic addresses 15ΓÇö22. VPU port 1 is shown on odd-numbered diagnostic addresses.
VPU port 2 is shown on even-numbered diagnostic addresses. For example, VPU port 1 is shown on
diagnostic address (DA) 15. VPU port 2 is shown on DA 16.
2.

Review the information listed for each VPU port. Address any issues based on the data given in the
camera diagnostic information.
Camera diagnostic information is listed for each VPU port as seven digits separated by commas. For
example, DA 15 = 1,1,2,3,0,0,0.
3.
First Digit = Expected Camera Connection
ΓÇó
0 = No Camera Expected on This Port
ΓÇó
1 = Camera Expected on This Port
ΓÇó
Second Digit = Camera Connection Status
ΓÇó
0 = No Camera Connected to This Port
ΓÇó
1 = Camera Connected to This Port
ΓÇó
Third Digit = Camera Current in Milliamps (mA)
ΓÇó
Fourth Digit = Link Health
ΓÇó
0 = Not Connected
ΓÇó
1 = Good Link
ΓÇó
2 = Fair Link
ΓÇó
3 - Bad Link
ΓÇó
Fifth Digit = Always a Value of 0
Sixth Digit = Always a Value of 0
Seventh Digit = Always a Value of 0

being acknowledged. The See & Spray system is disabled.
NOTE:

Schematic:
Overview: CAN and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30D)
See & Spray Addressing ΓÇöElectrical Schematic(240-30L)
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Disconnect the machine battery and reconnect the machine battery. This process reboots the system,
clears out error messages, and re-establishes proper communication messages.
1.
Verify that the DTC is still active. Refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05). If
Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
inactive, refer to 
 (Section 211, Group 05).
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
2.
Utilize Service ADVISOR to ensure that the appropriate control unit has the proper software. Update
software as necessary. Refer toControl Unit Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
3.
Ensure that all control units are communicating on the applicable network or subnet.
4.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > verify that message counts are
changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
1.
If faulty communication network has another application on the display, then refer to the
display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable network > verify that all of the necessary
components show up as available.
2.
Utilize the display information and the schematic to narrow down the possible locations for the
fault.
3.
Refer to the component locations.
5.
Visually check all connectors, components, and harnesses for corrosion, binding, and damage.
1.
Verify that harness and circuit routing match the electrical schematic.
2.
Refer to the electrical schematic.
6.
Visually verify that relevant fuses are not blown.
1.
Identify relevant power and ground/return circuits.
2.

 (Section 240, Group 10).

If the test between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit is bad, and the test between
the power circuit and the frame ground is bad, then the issue is on the power circuit.
3.
Refer to the electrical schematic. Narrow down any circuit issues to the root cause. Refer to Circuit
 (Section 240, Group 10).
Checks
8.
Reprogram the control unit. Refer toControl Unit Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
9.
Refer to the electrical system diagnostic.
10.

======================================================================
MATCH 'See & Spray Addressing' on PDF page 4356
======================================================================
4356
[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa860fda2b]
See & Spray Addressing ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
Component Information
NOTE:
Wake-up power requires the engine running.
After the VPU receives wake-up power, the VPU begins the addressing process, which can take up to two minutes to complete. The addressing process utilizes
increasing electrical frequencies at each VPU. This allows the VPUs to identify their relative position on the boom. This relative position is used to create unique
identifiers for each VPU. These unique identifiers are then utilized whenever the VPUs report through the various controller area network (CAN) binary unit system
(bus) circuits. This enables communication on both the BoomTrac CAN bus and the respective ExactApply nozzle subnet CAN bus.
Addressing requires proper function of several circuits to complete:
Vision processing unit (VPU) assemblies
ΓÇó
W0087 ΓÇö Center Frame Sensing Harness (249-40W)
ΓÇó

ΓÇó

ΓÇó
Each VPU addresses with a number starting from left and going to the right.
ΓÇó
The VPU addressing circuits are connected in series. These circuits address from the two inside VPUs 
then flow out to either side.
ΓÇó
Specifically, addressing to the left of the operator's seat starts with VPU 5 and addressing circuits flow 
out to VPU 1 on the left.
ΓÇó
Specifically, addressing to the right of the operator's seat starts with VPU L and addressing circuits flow 
out to VPU A on the right.
ΓÇó

[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa860ee76d]
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
Component Information
Controller Area Network binary unit system (CAN bus) systems transfer messages between every control
unit on the machine. These messages include commands, requests, broadcast information, responses,
acknowledgements, and various group functions. The CAN bus is a linear network, and the control units
are connected along its length in series.
The operator has no direct control of the CAN bus systems. CAN bus operates automatically whenever the
key switch is in the ACC, RUN, or START positions.
Can Circuits
Digital messages transfer information from control unit to control unit along two circuits. One control unit
sends a message, and that message is either addressed to another control unit or to all control units. The
receiving control unit uses the information in the message to monitor or control the machine.
Each CAN bus network includes a 2.5-3.5 V CAN Hi circuit (yellow wire) and a 1.5-2.5 V CAN Lo circuit (green
wire). When no messages are being transmitted on the CAN bus, CAN bus high and CAN bus low circuits
are both approximately 2.5 V. When messages are being sent between the control units, the CAN bus high
voltage increases, and the CAN bus low voltage decreases.
CAN Terminators
Vision processing unit (VPU) assemblies
ΓÇó
A725 ΓÇö Gateway control unit (GWC)
ΓÇó

MATCH 'See & Spray CAN Bus' on PDF page 4361
======================================================================
4361
The NZCs on the subnets communicate with the MNC via the CAN bus. The ExactApply CAN bus system
runs in series (pass-through communication) and passes through each NZC to the next NZC. Nozzle lights
are located on the bottom of each NZC. Good CAN bus communication is required to turn ON and OFF each
light. The NZCs must have good power and ground for their lights to function properly. Without good
power and ground, the lights do not work at all. If an NZC loses power, or power is interrupted, after the
lights initially turn ON, CAN information from the other NZC outboard remain in communication with the
master control unit (the lights will remain on).
NOTE:
When testing CAN bus communication, verify good connection and communication from the
machine to the nozzle system.
Related Information
Subnet 4 (CAN bus circuit 3944 and 3945 on the far right boom)

4362
[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85b748b6]
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Theory of 
Operation
Component Information
See & Spray Cameras and FAKRA Cables
Each See & Spray camera sends video information to a vision processing unit (VPU), using a non-repairable
Fachkreis Automobil (FAKRA) cable with integrated non-repairable connectors. FAKRA cables allow up to 6
GHz of information to be transmitted. FAKRA connectors mechanically lock to ensure a secure connection.
More See & Spray camera information, including how and when to calibrate these cameras, is provided in
the appropriate OperatorΓÇÖs Manual (OM).
VPUs
VPUs 1, 2, 5, and 6 connect to eight FAKRA cables and eight See & Spray cameras respectively. VPU 3
connects to four FAKRA cables and four See & Spray cameras. Each VPU processes the necessary
information and sends that information along to the appropriate See & Spray Ethernet switch, using an
Ethernet cable. The VPU periodically verifies that all of these FAKRA and Ethernet connections are
functioning correctly.
Each VPU also communicates necessary camera information through the BoomTrac CAN bus to the
gateway control unit (GWC) and through the ExactApply CAN bus to the master nozzle control unit (MNC).
This camera data provides the information necessary to automatically adjust the ExactApply nozzles (NZCs).
See & Spray Ethernet Switches and Ethernet Cables
See & Spray Ethernet switch 1 and 2, connects to four VPUs through four Ethernet cables. See & Spray
Ethernet switch 1 and 2 process the necessary information and act as a hub for See and Spray camera data.
Twisted pair circuits provide extra signal protection from magnetic influences. Ethernet cables provide
high-speed communication, which allows real-time adjustments. When needed, these Ethernet cables
provide the means to reprogram the VPUs. These Ethernet cables and integrated connectors are non-
repairable.
Ethernet Configuration 2
See & Spray Ethernet switch 1 & 2 transmits all of the See & Spray camera data information along to
configuration 2 Ethernet switch assembly, using two twisted pair Ethernet cables.
Configuration 2 Ethernet switch assembly also receives camera data from the right-hand camera and the
left-hand cameras when equipped on the machine. Each connection is made with a twisted pair of circuits.
This information plus the See & Spray camera information is sent to configuration 1 Ethernet switch
assembly, using two twisted pair Ethernet cables.
A124 ΓÇö Configuration 1 Ethernet switch assembly
ΓÇó
A207 ΓÇö Configuration 2 Ethernet switch assembly
ΓÇó
A726 ΓÇö Ethernet switch 1
ΓÇó
A727 ΓÇö Ethernet switch 2
ΓÇó
Vision processing cameras
ΓÇó
Vision processing unit (VPU) assemblies
ΓÇó

MATCH 'See & Spray Ethernet' on PDF page 4363

4363
Ethernet Configuration 1
Configuration 1 Ethernet switch assembly sends the necessary camera data along to the modular
telematics gateway (MTG), to the integrated StarFire control unit, and to the premium server. These three
control units connect to various CAN buses, which allows needed See & Spray camera information to
report to all other control units connected through those CAN networks.
Related Information

MATCH 'GWC ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation' on PDF page 5681

5681
[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa861a9cfa]
GWC ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The gateway control unit (GWC) enables the See & Spray application within the display. The GWC is the
bridge between the implement controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) and the See & Spray
components. If the GWC is offline, or not on the machine's implement CAN bus, the See & Spray page and
run page modules are grayed out with See & Spray disabled.
Component Information:
Unswitched power is a constant 12 V power supply that comes directly from the batteries and battery
disconnect switch. A system fuse within the battery box protects the supplied power circuit to the rear mid-
chassis cab load center. The MNC/See & Spray control unit fuse within the rear mid-chassis load center
protects the supplied power circuit to the ExactApply load center. Power is then supplied to the See &
Spray load center.
Unswitched power is provided through the gateway and Ethernet switch power fuse within the See & Spray
load center.
Switched power for the system to wake up the control units, as well as begin the proper shutdown
procedure of the control units, comes from the power train domain control unit (PODC). The PODC receives
the wakeup signal from the operator station domain control unit (OSDC). The PODC then sends 12 V to the
GWC. The GWC then relays wakeup and shutdown signals to the Ethernet switch and the vision processing
unit(s) (VPUs) through the GWC.
The GWC acts as a gateway to connect the implement CAN bus and See & Spray/BoomTrac CAN bus. The
VPUs' vision spray modules (VSMs) communicate status messages to the GWC. The GWC monitors
messages on both busses and relays the necessary information to necessary control units on the
implement bus.
The GWC allows the operator interface with VPUs VSMs and cameras on the boom through the See & Spray
screen on the display. The user can select a specific VPU VSM and camera lens. It also provides visual
feedback to what the camera is sensing by sending the information from the VPU and broadcasting the
video through ethernet from VPU to the ethernet switches and then back to the Generation 4 Display.
A725 ΓÇö Gateway control unit
ΓÇó
F80-5 ΓÇöSee & Spray load center
ΓÇó

ΓÇó

ΓÇó

ΓÇó

Review all of the vision processing units (VPUs) and their associated camera video feeds.
Is there a problem with one or random camera video feeds?
Result:
YES:
To diagnose individual cameras, refer to 
 (240-50L).
See & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic
NO:
There are multiple vision processing units (VPUs) with problems.
Refer to 
 (240-50L).

Retest the See & Spray system.
Does the problem move from the original faulted harness location to the new location?

8┬á Procedure
Action:
IMPORTANT:
After performing this procedure, verify that the pins tested in this step are still fully seated and
functional. Damage to pins can cause component failure.
Refer to theSee & Spray Ethernet and FAKRA ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30L), theBoom CAN Bus ΓÇö
Electrical Schematic(240-30D) and theExactApply CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30D).
Identify the faulty vision processing unit (VPU) assembly 1 connector (XA7XX-1).
Controller Area Network (CAN) high voltage test:
Refer to theSee & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30L) for testing VPU CAN high circuits.
While connected, measure the voltage between XA7XX-1 ExactApply (EA) CAN high circuit 39X4 (pin A3) and
frame ground.
If available, while connected, measure the voltage between XA7XX-1 EA CAN high circuit 39X4 (pin B3) and
frame ground.


======================================================================
MATCH 'See & Spray CAN Bus' on PDF page 5915
======================================================================
5915
While connected, measure the voltage between XA7XX-1 BoomTrac CAN high circuit 9964 (pin F3) and
frame ground.
CAN binary unit system (bus) high voltage should be 2.5ΓÇö4.0 V.
CAN low voltage test:
Refer to theSee & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30L) for testing VPU CAN low circuits.
While connected, measure the voltage between XA7XX-1 EA CAN low circuit 39X5 (pin A4) and frame
ground.
If available, while connected, measure the voltage between XA7XX-1 EA CAN low circuit 39X5 (pin B4) and
frame ground.
While connected, measure the voltage between XA7XX-1 BoomTrac CAN low circuit 9965 (pin F4) and frame
ground.
CAN bus low voltage should be 1.0ΓÇö2.5 V.
Are the voltages withing specification?
Result:
YES:
GO TO9
NO:
This result indicates that there is a controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (BUS) issue.
To diagnose ExactApply CAN bus issues, refer to 
 (240-50L) and
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic
theExactApply CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(240-30D).

 (240-50D).

10┬á Procedure
Action:
IMPORTANT:
After performing this procedure, but before proceeding to the next procedure, verify that the
pins tested in this step are still fully seated and functional. Damage to pins can cause
component failure.
NOTE:
Set-up this procedure so that measurements are taken within 5 seconds of turning the machine
key switch ON. This procedure will require two technicians or test leads long enough to reach
the cab.
Do not use the black lead on the multimeter. Using the black lead and connecting to ground
can cause a false positive reading.
On the left addressing subnet, the first faulted vision processing unit (VPU) would be the
highest numbered VPU reporting as faulted. On the right addressing subnet the first faulted
VPU will be the lowest numbered VPU reporting as faulted.
Identify the first faulted VPU closest to the center of the machine by navigating to the See & Spray
application.
Key switch in the OFF position.
Identify the corresponding VPU assembly 1 connector (XA7XX-1).
With the connector still connected, connect the red lead of the multimeter to the appropriate address sync
in (pin D3) on the faulty VPU assembly 1 connector.
Key switch on the RUN position, engine running.
Measure and record the frequency.
Allow 2ΓÇö3 minutes for the VPUs to finish addressing.
Refer to the Frequency Table located in the 
 (240-
See & Spray Addressing ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
20L).
Is the frequency within specification?
Result:
YES:
GO TO12
NO:
GO TO11
┬á
11┬á Procedure
Action:


======================================================================
MATCH 'See & Spray Addressing' on PDF page 5917

5917
IMPORTANT:
After performing this procedure, but before proceeding to the next procedure, verify that the
pins tested in this step are still fully seated and functional. Damage to pins can cause
component failure.
Key switch in the OFF position.
Move to the next vision processing unit (VPU) closer to the center of the machine.
Identify the appropriate vision processing unit (VPU) assembly 1 connector (XA7XX-1).
With the connector still connected, connect the red lead of the multimeter to the appropriate address sync
out (pin C3) on the VPU assembly 1 connector.
Key switch on the RUN position, engine running.
Measure and record the frequency.
Allow 2ΓÇö3 minutes for the VPUs to finish addressing.
Refer to the Frequency Table located in the 
 (240-
See & Spray Addressing ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
20L).
Is the frequency within specification?
Result:
YES:
This result indicates that the vision processing unit (VPU) inward of the first faulted VPU is properly 
outputting the address sync signal.
To continue diagnosing an issue with the addressing circuits or the VPU, refer to the See & Spray
 (240-50L).
Addressing ΓÇö Electrical Diagnostic
NO:
This result indicates that the vision processing unit (VPU) inward of the first faulted VPU is not outputting 
the address sync signal.
GO TO13

out (pin C3) on the faulty VPU assembly 1 connector.
Key switch in the RUN position, engine running.
Measure and record the frequency.

13┬á Procedure
Action:
Key switch in the OFF position.
Swap the faulty vision processing unit (VPU) with a known properly functioning VPU.
Retest the See & Spray system.
Does the problem move from the original faulted VPU location to the new location?
Result:
YES:
Replace the faulty vision processing unit (VPU).
Refer to the appropriate RTM.
If replacing VPU 4 (A714), move one of the follower VPUs to the lead position and install a new VPU in the
follower location.
NO:
This result indicates a possible address sync circuit issue.
To continue diagnosing and address syncing issue, refer to theSee & Spray Addressing ΓÇö Electrical
Schematic(240-50L) to continue diagnostics.
